# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

Calculates the total budget of all projects in all departments of this company.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalBudget to 0.
2. For each department in departments:
   a. For each project in department.projects:
      i. Add project.budget to totalBudget.
3. Return totalBudget.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.projects
- Project.budget
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project

- None.

- double: sum of budget values of every project in every department.
- The value is non-negative if all project budgets are non-negative.

- The company exists.

- Each department.projects collection is not null.
- Each project budget is defined.
Post-condition:
- Returns the exact sum of all project budgets across all departments.

Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one production project in the company.
An employee is counted once even if they work on multiple production projects.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty set distinctEmployees.
2. For each department in departments:
   a. For each project in department.projects:
      i. If the project is a ProductionProject:
         - For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
           Add the employee to distinctEmployees.
3. Return the size of distinctEmployees.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.projects
- Project.workingEmployees
- ProductionProject
- Employee.ID
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input:
- None.
Output:
- int: number of distinct employees participating in at least one production project.
- The result is greater than or equal to 0.
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- departments is not null.
- Each department.projects collection is not null.
- Each project's workingEmployees collection is not null when the project is a production project.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary:
Calculates the total budget of all projects in all departments of this company.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalBudget to 0.
2. For each department in departments:
   a. For each project in department.projects:
      i. Add project.budget to totalBudget.
3. Return totalBudget.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.projects
- Project.budget
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project
Input:
- None.
Output:
- double: sum of budget values of every project in every department.
- The value is non-negative if all project budgets are non-negative.
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- departments is not null.
- Each department.projects collection is not null.
- Each project budget is defined.
Post-condition:
- Returns the exact sum of all project budgets across all departments.
- The company state is not modified.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary:
Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one production project in the company.
An employee is counted once even if they work on multiple production projects.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty set distinctEmployees.
2. For each department in departments:
   a. For each project in department.projects:
      i. If the project is a ProductionProject:
         - For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
           Add the employee to distinctEmployees.
3. Return the size of distinctEmployees.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.projects
- Project.workingEmployees
- ProductionProject
- Employee.ID
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input:
- None.
Output:
- int: number of distinct employees participating in at least one production project.
- The result is greater than or equal to 0.
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- departments is not null.
- Each department.projects collection is not null.
- Each project's workingEmployees collection is not null when the project is a production project.
Post-condition:
- Returns the count of unique employees assigned to any production project in the company.
- The company state is not modified.

Company::addDepartment
Summary:
Adds a new department to this company if doing so keeps the company department count within the allowed range
[2, 8] and the department name is unique in the company.
Algorithm:
1. Validate the input department is not null.
2. Validate the department name is not null or empty after trimming.
3. Check whether another department in departments already has the same name.
4. If the name is duplicated, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. If the current number of departments is already 8, throw IllegalStateException.
6. Add the department to departments.
7. Set the department's company reference to this company.
8. Keep bidirectional consistency between Company.departments and Department.company.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.company
- Department.ID
- Department.email
Related Classes: Company, Department
Input:
- department: Department to be added.
- The department must have a non-null, non-empty name representation; since the diagram does not define a name attribute, the unique department identity is inferred from Department.ID.
- The department must not already belong to this company.
Output:
- void.
Pre-condition:
- This company exists.
- Current departments count is between 2 and 8 inclusive.
- Input department is not null.
- Department identifier/name used for uniqueness is not null or empty.
- No existing department in this company has the same identifier/name.
Post-condition:
- If successful, the input department is contained in Company.departments.
- The input department.company refers to this company.
- The number of departments increases by 1 and remains <= 8.
- If any constraint is violated, no state change occurs and an exception is thrown.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary:
Removes an existing department from this company by department name, provided the minimum department count constraint is preserved.
Algorithm:
1. Validate the input name is not null or empty after trimming.
2. Search departments for a department whose unique name/identifier matches the input name.
3. If no matching department exists, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. If the current number of departments is 2, throw IllegalStateException.
5. Remove the matching department from departments.
6. Clear the removed department's company reference.
7. Return the name of the removed department.
Related Features:
- Company.departments
- Department.company
- Department.ID
Related Classes: Company, Department
Input:
- name: String identifying the department to remove.
- The name must be non-null and non-empty.
- The name must match an existing department's unique identifier/name; since the diagram does not define Department.name, Department.ID is used as the inferred unique department key.
Output:
- String: the name/identifier of the removed department.
Pre-condition:
- This company exists.
- Current number of departments is between 2 and 8 inclusive.
- name is not null and not empty.
- A department matching the given name exists.
- Removing one department will not reduce the company below 2 departments.
Post-condition:
- If successful, the matching department is removed from Company.departments.
- The removed department.company becomes null or otherwise detached from this company.
- The number of departments decreases by 1 and remains >= 2.
- If any constraint is violated, no state change occurs and an exception is thrown.

The production projects are characterized by a site code.

```text
functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary:
Calculates the average budget amount of all projects in this department.
Algorithm:
1. If projects is empty, return 0.0 or an equivalent neutral average value.
2. Initialize sum to 0 and count to 0.
3. For each project in projects:
   a. Add project.budget to sum.
   b. Increment count.
4. Return sum / count.
Related Features:
- Department.projects
- Project.budget
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input:
- None.
Output:
- double: average budget of all projects in the department.
- If there are no projects, the result is defined as 0.0.
Pre-condition:
- The department exists.
- projects is not null.
- Each project budget is defined.
Post-condition:
- Returns the arithmetic mean of all project budgets in this department.
- The department state is not modified.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary:
Retrieves the funding group type of all community projects in this department.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty list result.
2. For each project in projects:
   a. If the project is a CommunityProject:
      i. Obtain project.fundingGroup.type.
      ii. Append the type to result.
3. Return result.
Related Features:
- Department.projects
- CommunityProject.fundingGroup
- FundingGroup.type
- FundingGroupType
Related Classes: Department, Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input:
- None.
Output:
- List<FundingGroupType>: ordered list of funding group types corresponding to all community projects in this department.
- The list may be empty if the department has no community projects.
Pre-condition:
- The department exists.
- projects is not null.
- Every CommunityProject has a non-null fundingGroup with a defined type.
Post-condition:
- Returns a list containing the funding group type of each community project in the department.
- The department state is not modified.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.
```

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
```

  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    if (name == null || name.trim().isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("name must not be null or empty");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() <= 2)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("minimum number of departments reached");
    }
    Department target = null;
    for (Department department : getDepartments())
    {
      if (name.equals(department.getID()))
      {

        break;
      }
    }
    if (target == null)

      throw new IllegalArgumentException("no department found with name: " + name);
    }
    getDepartments().remove(target);
    target.setCompany(null);
    return target.getID();
    
  }

- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Calculates the average budget amount of all projects in this department.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If projects is empty, return 0.0 or an equivalent neutral average value.
   * 2. Initialize sum to 0 and count to 0.
   * 3. For each project in projects:
   *    a. Add project.budget to sum.
   *    b. Increment count.
   * 4. Return sum / count.
   * Related Features:
   * - Department.projects
   * - Project.budget
   * Related Classes: Department, Project
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - double: average budget of all projects in the department.
   * - If there are no projects, the result is defined as 0.0.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The department exists.
   * - projects is not null.
   * - Each project budget is defined.
   * Post-condition:
   * - Returns the arithmetic mean of all project budgets in this department.
   * - The department state is not modified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageBudget()
  {
    if (getProjects().isEmpty())
    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double sum = 0.0;
    int count = 0;
    for (Project project : getProjects())
    {
      sum += project.getBudget();
      count++;
    }
    return count == 0 ? 0.0 : sum / count;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}

    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

An employee appearing in multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty set distinctEmployees.

   b. Add each employee to distinctEmployees.
5. Return the size of distinctEmployees.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary:
Computes the average budget of all projects in the department.
Algorithm:
1. If department.projects is empty, return 0.0.
2. Initialize sum to 0 and count to 0.
3. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
4. Add project.budget to sum and increment count.
5. Return sum / count.
Related Features:
- projects
- Project.budget
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input:
- None.
Output:
- double
  - The arithmetic mean of the budgets of all projects in this department.
  - Returns 0.0 if the department has no projects.
Pre-condition:
- The department exists.
- department.projects is initialized.
Post-condition:
- No model state is modified.
- The returned value equals the average of all project budgets in the department.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary:
Retrieves the funding group types of all CommunityProject instances in the department.
Only community projects are considered.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty list result.
2. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
3. If the project is a CommunityProject:
   a. Access the project's fundingGroup.
   b. Append fundingGroup.type to result.
4. Return result.
Related Features:
- projects
- Project.department
- CommunityProject.fundingGroup
- FundingGroup.type
- FundingGroupType
Related Classes: Department, Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input:
- None.
Output:
- List<FundingGroupType>
  - A list of funding group types corresponding to all community projects in the department.
  - The list may be empty if no community projects exist.
  - Duplicates are allowed if multiple community projects share the same funding group type.
Pre-condition:
- The department exists.
- department.projects is initialized.
- Every CommunityProject in the list has a non-null fundingGroup with a non-null type.
Post-condition:
- No model state is modified.
- The returned list contains the funding group type of every community project in the department, in iteration order.

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary:
Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one ProductionProject in the company.
An employee appearing in multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty set distinctEmployees.
2. Iterate over each Department in company.departments.
3. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
4. If the project is a ProductionProject:
   a. Iterate over project.workingEmployees.
   b. Add each employee to distinctEmployees.
5. Return the size of distinctEmployees.
Related Features:
- departments
- Department.projects
- Project.workingEmployees
- Employee.ID
- Employee.email
- Employee.name
- Employee.number
- ProductionProject
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input:
- None.
Output:
- int
  - The number of distinct employees participating in at least one production project.
  - Returns 0 if there are no production projects or no working employees.
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- company.departments and all department.projects lists are initialized.
- For each production project, workingEmployees is initialized.
Post-condition:
- No model state is modified.
- The returned integer is the cardinality of the set of employees involved in production projects.

Company::addDepartment
Summary:
Adds a new department to the company if the department name is unique and the company currently has fewer than 8 departments.
Algorithm:
1. If department is null, or department.name is null, empty, or blank, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. If company.departments already contains a department with the same name, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If company.departments.size() is 8, throw IllegalStateException.
4. Add the department to company.departments.
5. Set department.company to this company.
Related Features:
- departments
- Department.company
Related Classes: Company, Department
Input:
- department : Department
  - The department to be added.
  - The department must have a unique non-null, non-empty name.
Output:
- void
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- company.departments is initialized.
- The company currently has between 2 and 7 departments inclusive before adding.
- The input department is not null and has a valid name.
- No existing department in the company has the same name as the input department.
Post-condition:
- The department is added to the company if and only if the company size remains within the allowed range.
- company.departments.size() increases by 1.
- The added department.company refers to this company.
- If the operation violates the department-name or maximum-size constraints, an exception is thrown and the model state is unchanged.

6. Set the removed department.company to null.

- A department with the given name exists in the company.

- company.departments.size() decreases by 1.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary:
Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one ProductionProject in the company.
An employee appearing in multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm:
1. Create an empty set distinctEmployees.
2. Iterate over each Department in company.departments.
3. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
4. If the project is a ProductionProject:
   a. Iterate over project.workingEmployees.
   b. Add each employee to distinctEmployees.
5. Return the size of distinctEmployees.
Related Features:
- departments
- Department.projects
- Project.workingEmployees
- Employee.ID
- Employee.email
- Employee.name
- Employee.number
- ProductionProject
Related Classes: Company, Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input:
- None.
Output:
- int
  - The number of distinct employees participating in at least one production project.
  - Returns 0 if there are no production projects or no working employees.
Pre-condition:
- The company exists.
- company.departments and all department.projects lists are initialized.
- For each production project, workingEmployees is initialized.
Post-condition:
- No model state is modified.
- The returned integer is the cardinality of the set of employees involved in production projects.

Company::addDepartment
Summary:
Adds a new department to the company if the department name is unique and the company currently has fewer than 8 departments.
Algorithm:

4. Add the department to company.departments.
5. Set department.company to this company.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * Summary:
   * Computes the total budget of all projects in all departments of the company.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Initialize totalBudget to 0.
   * 2. Iterate over each Department in company.departments.
   * 3. For each department, iterate over its projects list.
   * 4. Add each project.budget to totalBudget.
   * 5. Return totalBudget.
```

   * @generated NOT

  public double calculateTotalBudget()
  {
    double totalBudget = 0.0;
    for (Department department : getDepartments())
    {
      for (Project project : department.getProjects())

        totalBudget += project.getBudget();
      }
    }
    return totalBudget;

   * Summary:

  public int countEmployeesInProductionProjects()
  {
    Set<Employee> distinctEmployees = new HashSet<Employee>();
    for (Department department : getDepartments())
    {
      for (Project project : department.getProjects())
      {
        if (project instanceof ProductionProject)
        {
          distinctEmployees.addAll(project.getWorkingEmployees());
        }
      }
    }
    return distinctEmployees.size();
    
  }

  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    if (department == null || department.getID() == null || department.getID().trim().isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException();
    }
    if (department.getEmail() == null || department.getEmail().trim().isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException();
    }
    for (Department existingDepartment : getDepartments())
    {
      if (department.getID().equals(existingDepartment.getID()))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException();
      }
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() == 8)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException();
    }
    getDepartments().add(department);
    department.setCompany(this);
    
  }

  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    if (name == null || name.trim().isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException();
    }
    Department targetDepartment = null;
    for (Department department : getDepartments())
    {
      if (name.equals(department.getID()))
      {
        targetDepartment = department;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (targetDepartment == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException();
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() == 2)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException();
    }
    getDepartments().remove(targetDepartment);
    targetDepartment.setCompany(null);
    return targetDepartment.getID();

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Computes the average budget of all projects in the department.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If department.projects is empty, return 0.0.
   * 2. Initialize sum to 0 and count to 0.
   * 3. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
   * 4. Add project.budget to sum and increment count.
   * 5. Return sum / count.
   * Related Features:
   * - projects
   * - Project.budget
   * Related Classes: Department, Project
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - double
   *   - The arithmetic mean of the budgets of all projects in this department.
   *   - Returns 0.0 if the department has no projects.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The department exists.
   * - department.projects is initialized.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No model state is modified.
   * - The returned value equals the average of all project budgets in the department.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageBudget()
  {
    if (projects == null || projects.isEmpty())
    {
      return 0.0;
    }

    double sum = 0.0;
    int count = 0;
    for (Project project : projects)
    {
      sum += project.getBudget();
      count++;
    }
    return count == 0 ? 0.0 : sum / count;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Retrieves the funding group types of all CommunityProject instances in the department.
   * Only community projects are considered.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Create an empty list result.
   * 2. Iterate over each Project in department.projects.
   * 3. If the project is a CommunityProject:
   *    a. Access the project's fundingGroup.
   *    b. Append fundingGroup.type to result.
   * 4. Return result.
   * Related Features:
   * - projects
   * - Project.department
   * - CommunityProject.fundingGroup
   * - FundingGroup.type
   * - FundingGroupType
   * Related Classes: Department, Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - List<FundingGroupType>
   *   - A list of funding group types corresponding to all community projects in the department.
   *   - The list may be empty if no community projects exist.
   *   - Duplicates are allowed if multiple community projects share the same funding group type.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The department exists.
   * - department.projects is initialized.
   * - Every CommunityProject in the list has a non-null fundingGroup with a non-null type.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No model state is modified.
   * - The returned list contains the funding group type of every community project in the department, in iteration order.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model kind="operation"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<FundingGroupType> getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects()
  {
    EList<FundingGroupType> result = new BasicEList<FundingGroupType>();
    if (projects == null || projects.isEmpty())
    {
      return result;
    }

    for (Project project : projects)
    {
      if (project instanceof CommunityProject)
      {
        CommunityProject communityProject = (CommunityProject)project;
        result.add(communityProject.getFundingGroup().getType());
      }
    }
    return result;
    
  }
